|P(e_i)|² = 1 − v_i²/|v|².
For i≠j: |P(e_i)∧P(e_j)|² = (1−v_i²/|v|²)(1−v_j²/|v|²) − (v_i v_j/|v|²)² = 1 − (v_i²+v_j²)/|v|² = v_k²/|v|². ✓. So |P(e_i)∧P(e_j)| = |v_k|/|v|.

In HM I believe the relevant norm IS the quotient norm: for x ∈ V = ℝⁿ/ℝv, ||π(x)||_V := dist_∞(x, ℝv) = inf_t ||x − tv||_∞. This is a norm on V (quotient norm of ℓ_∞). Then:

δ(v) = min_{z∈ℤⁿ} dist_∞(ℝv, z+½𝟙) = min_z ||π(z + ½𝟙)||_V = dist_V(c, Λ) where c = π(½𝟙).

OH WAIT. That's it! δ(v) = the distance (in quotient norm) from the point c = π(½𝟙) to the lattice Λ. Because π(z + ½𝟙) = π(z) + c ranges over Λ + c as z ranges over ℤⁿ, and ||π(z+½𝟙)||_V = dist_∞(z + ½𝟙, ℝv) = dist_∞(ℝv, z + ½𝟙) [symmetric]. 

So δ(v) = dist_V(c, Λ) — the depth of the "center hole": how far the projected cube-center is from the projected lattice.

PRACTICAL EXACT ALGORITHM via the FUNDAMENTAL DOMAIN + LP/MILP or via "rounding" certification:

python
 
  
 
 
from fractions import Fraction
from itertools import combinations
import math

# ---------- Eulerian polynomials ----------
def eulerian_poly(k):  # A_k(z), coefficients list from z^0..z^{k-1}; A_0 = [1]
    if k == 0: return [1]
    # Eulerian numbers <k, j> for j=0..k-1
    A = [[1]]
    for m in range(1, k+1):
        prev = A[-1]  # length m (for m-1: indices 0..m-2)... 
 
 

Hmm — Eulerian numbers: A(n, m) for n ≥ 1, m = 0..n−1: A(n,m) = (m+1)A(n−1,m) + (n−m)A(n−1,m−1). Let me just hardcode via recurrence: 
python
 
  
 
 
def eulerian_table(N):
    # E[n][m], n from 1..N, m 0..n-1
    E = {1: [1]}
    for n in range(2, N+1):
        prev = E[n-1]  # length n-1
        row = []
        for m in range(n):
            a = (m+1)*prev[m] if m < len(prev) else 0
            b = (n-m)*prev[m-1] if m-1 >= 0 else 0
            row.append(a+b)
        E[n] = row
    return E
 
 

A_1 = [1] ✓ (z); A_2 = [1,1]·... E[2]: m=0: (0+1)·prev[0] = 1·1 = 1 + (2−0)·prev[−1]·... m−1 = −1: 0: row = [1, m=1: (1+1)prev[1]·... prev has len 1: m=1 ≥ len: a = 0; b = (2−1)·prev[0] = 1: row = [1, 1] ✓ (z + z²) ✓. A_3: E[3]: prev = [1,1]: m=0: 1·1 = 1 + 0 = 1; m=1: 2·prev[1] = 2 + 2·prev[0] = 2: 4; m=2: 0 + 1·prev[1] = 1: [1,4,1] ✓. A_4: prev = [1,4,1]: m=0: 1; m=1: 2·4 + 3·1 = 11; m=2: 3·1 + 2·4 = 11; m=3: 0 + 1·1 = 1: [1,11,11,1] ✓✓.

DFS count: 
python
 
  
 
 
def dfs_count(v, t):
    # count lines (cosets of R v in Q^n with integer points, canonical rep u with u_1 in [0, v_1)) meeting [0, t]^n
    # u_1 fixed in {0..v_1-1}; DFS over u_2..u_n
    n = len(v); v1 = v[0]
    count = 0
    # window in parameter s: line u + s v; coord j: u_j + s v_j in [0, t]
    # maintain W = [lo, hi] (Fraction) over fixed coords
    def rec(j, lo, hi):
        nonlocal count
        if j == n: 
            count += 1  # W nonempty guaranteed by caller
            return
        # u_j must satisfy: exists s in [lo, hi] with u_j + s v_j in [0, t]
        # s range: [ (0 - u_j)/v_j , (t - u_j)/v_j ]  (v_j > 0)
        # exists s in [lo,hi] ∩ that:  ⟺  (0 - u_j)/v_j <= hi  and  (t - u_j)/v_j >= lo
        #   ⟺ u_j >= -hi*v_j  and  u_j <= t - lo*v_j
        lb = -hi * v[j]; ub = t - lo * v[j]
        uj_min = math.ceil(lb); uj_max = math.floor(ub)
        for uj in range(uj_min, uj_max+1):
            nlo = max(lo, Fraction(-uj, v[j])); nhi = min(hi, Fraction(t - uj, v[j]))
            if nlo <= nhi: rec(j+1, nlo, nhi)
    for u1 in range(0, v1):
        lo = Fraction(-u1, v[0]); hi = Fraction(t - u1, v[0])
        rec(1, lo, hi)
    return count

λ exact: 
python
 
  
 
 
def lambda_exact(v):
    # candidates: t in [0,1]: 0, 1, breakpoints m/(2 v_j), crossovers m/(v_i+v_j), m/|v_i - v_j|
    n = len(v)
    cands = {Fraction(0), Fraction(1)}
    for j in range(n):
        for m in range(0, 2*v[j]+1):
            t = Fraction(m, 2*v[j])
            if 0 <= t <= 1: cands.add(t)
    for i in range(n):
        for j in range(i+1, n):
            for denom in (v[i]+v[j], abs(v[i]-v[j])):
                if denom == 0: continue
                for m in range(0, denom+1):
                    t = Fraction(m, denom)
                    if 0 <= t <= 1: cands.add(t)
    best = Fraction(0)
    for t in cands:
        m = min(dist_frac(t * vj) for vj in v)   # dist_frac(x) = |x - round| 
        if m > best: best = m; best_t = t
    return best, best_t

Repeatedly: pick two indices i≠j with cur[j] ≠ 0: apply row op: row_i = row_i − q·row_j with q = floor(cur[i]/cur[j])·... — standard Euclidean reduction to reduce cur to a unit vector e_k·... then swap rows to e₁. python
 
  
 
 
def smith_row_reduce(v):
    n = len(v)
    U = [[int(i==j) for j in range(n)] for i in range(n)]
    cur = list(v)
    # reduce: while there exist i>=1 with cur[i] != 0: use col-style Euclid on entries
    idx = 0  # target: make cur = e_0
    # Euclid: bring gcd to position 0 by ops on rows 0..n-1
    while True:
        nz = [i for i in range(n) if cur[i] != 0]
        if len(nz) == 1:
            break
        # let i = nz[0]... reduce others modulo cur[nz[0]]? better: pick pivot p = argmin |cur[i]| over nz
        p = min(nz, key=lambda i: abs(cur[i]))
        for i in nz:
            if i == p: continue
            q = cur[i] // cur[p]
            # row_i -= q * row_p
            for j in range(n): U[i][j] -= q * U[p][j]
            cur[i] -= q * cur[p]
        # after this all nz except p have cur[i] in [0, |cur[p|)... may still be nonzero; loop again (like Euclid)
    # now cur = c * e_p with c = ±1 (gcd=1)
    p = cur.index(next(x for x in cur if x != 0))
    assert abs(cur[p]) == 1
    # swap rows p and 0 if needed (and sign)
    if p != 0:
        for j in range(n): U[p][j], U[0][j] = U[0][j], U[p][j]
        cur[p], cur[0] = cur[0], cur[p]
    if cur[0] == -1:
        for j in range(n): U[0][j] = -U[0][j]
        cur[0] = 1
    assert all(cur[i] == 0 for i in range(1, n))
    assert cur[0] == 1
    detU = determinant(U)  # should be ±1... careful with the sign flip: flipping one row's sign changes det by -1: if det was -1 it becomes +1; if +1 becomes -1. Hmm — I want det(U) = ±1? For the construction: I need U invertible over Z (det ±1) ✓ any ±1 fine. Actually det ±1 both fine (unimodular). ✓
    return U  # U v = e_1
